Mathlib.RingTheory.AdicCompletion.Algebra

AdicCompletion.AdicCauchySequence.subalgebradefMathlib.RingTheory.AdicCompletion.Algebra, function or constant
Wording
the subalgebra of I (from the name)
English
Let $I$ be an ideal of $R$. Then the subalgebra of $I$ is a subalgebra of $R$ and the set of functions from natural numbers to elements of $R$.
Formula
$I \in \mathrm{Ideal} (R)\vdash \operatorname{subalgebra} (I)\in \mathrm{Subalgebra} (R , \Natural \rightarrow R)$
AdicCompletion.AdicCauchySequence.subringdefMathlib.RingTheory.AdicCompletion.Algebra, function or constant
Wording
the subring of I (from the docstring)
English
Let $I$ be an ideal of $R$. Then the subring of $I$ is a subring of the set of functions from natural numbers to elements of $R$ defined as the subring of the subalgebra of $I$.
Formula

AdicCompletion.liftAlgHomdefMathlib.RingTheory.AdicCompletion.Algebra, function or constant
Wording
the lift algebra homomorphism of I and f (from the name)
English
Let $I$ be an ideal of $S$. Let $f$ be a function from natural numbers $n_{1}$ to algebra homomorphisms of $A$ and the set of quotients of $S$ and $I ^ {n_{1}}$ such that for all natural numbers $m$ and $n$, for all elements $\mathrm{hle}$ of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to $\mathrm{Leq} (m , n)$, the composition of the factor of $R$ and $\mathrm{Ideal\_\_pow\_le\_pow\_right}$ applied to $\mathrm{hle}$ and $f$ applied to $n$ is equal to $f$ applied to $m$. Formula
$I \in \mathrm{Ideal} (S), f \in \Natural \rightarrow \mathrm{AlgHom} (R , A , \operatorname{Quotient} (S , I ^ {n_{1}})), \forall m \in \Natural . \forall n \in \Natural . \forall \mathrm{hle} \in \mathrm{Proof} (\mathrm{Leq} (m , n)). \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{comp} (\mathrm{Ideal\_\_Quotient\_\_factor\_u2090\_} (R , \mathrm{Ideal\_\_pow\_le\_pow\_right} (\mathrm{hle})), f (n))}{f (m)}\vdash \operatorname{liftAlgHom} (I , n_{1} , f , \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{AlgHom} (R , A , \mathrm{AdicCompletion} (I , S))$

AdicCompletion.IsAdicCauchydefMathlib.RingTheory.AdicCompletion.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $I$ be an ideal of $R$. Let $M$ be a set. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $\Natural$ to elements of $M$. Then $\operatorname{IsAdicCauchy}$ holds for $I$, $M$ and $f$, if for all elements $m$ and $n$ of $\Natural$, if $m \leq n$, then $\mathrm{SModEq}$ holds for $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {(I ^ {m})}{\operatorname{top}}$, $f$ applied to $m$ and $f$ applied to $n$.
Formula
$I \in \mathrm{Ideal} (R), M \in \mathrm{Set} , f \in \Natural \rightarrow M \vdash \operatorname{IsAdicCauchy} (I , M , f):\Leftrightarrow \forall m \in \Natural . \forall n \in \Natural . m \leq n \rightarrow \mathrm{SModEq} (\HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {(I ^ {m})}{\operatorname{top}}, f (m), f (n))$

IsTranscendenceBasisdefMathlib.RingTheory.AlgebraicIndependent.Defs, predicate
Wording
R is a transcendence basis (from the docstring)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Let $x_{4}$ be a function from elements of $x$ to elements of $A$. Then $R$ is a transcendence basis, if $\mathrm{AlgebraicIndependent}$ holds for $R$ and $x_{4}$ and for all elements $s$ of $\wp A$, if $\mathrm{AlgebraicIndepOn}$ holds for $R$, $\mathrm{id}$ and $s$, then if the range of $x_{4}$ is less than or equal to $s$, then the range of $x_{4}$ is equal to $s$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} , x_{4} \in x \rightarrow A \vdash \mathrm{IsTranscendenceBasis} (R , x_{4}):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{AlgebraicIndependent} (R , x_{4})\wedge (\forall s \in \wp A . \mathrm{AlgebraicIndepOn} (R , \mathrm{id} , s)\rightarrow \operatorname{range} (x_{4})\leq s \rightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{range} (x_{4})}{s})$

mkFactorOrderIsoOfFactorDvdEquivdefMathlib.RingTheory.ChainOfDivisors, function or constant
Wording
the order isomorphism between the factors of hd (from the docstring)
English
Assume that for all elements $l$ of $\SubtypeSet {l_{2}}{(\divides {l_{2}}{m})}$, for all elements $\mathrm{l'}$ of $\SubtypeSet {l_{1}}{(\divides {l_{1}}{m})}$, $\divides {d (l)}{d (\mathrm{l'})}$, if and only if $l$ divides $\mathrm{l'}$. Mathlib.RingTheory.DiscreteValuationRing.Basic

IsDiscreteValuationRingclassMathlib.RingTheory.DiscreteValuationRing.Basic, predicate
Wording
R is a discrete valuation ring (from the name)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Then we can say that $R$ is a discrete valuation ring.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{IsDiscreteValuationRing} (R)\ \mathsf{prop}$
IsDiscreteValuationRing.HasUnitMulPowIrreducibleFactorizationdefMathlib.RingTheory.DiscreteValuationRing.Basic, predicate
Wording
R has unit multiplicative power irreducible factorizations (from the name)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Then $R$ has unit multiplicative power irreducible factorizations, if there exists an element $p$ of $R$, such that $p$ is irreducible and for all elements $x_{1}$ of $R$, if $x_{1} \neq 0$, then there exists a natural number $n$ such that $\mathrm{Associated}$ holds for $p ^ {n}$ and $x_{1}$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{HasUnitMulPowIrreducibleFactorization} (R):\Leftrightarrow \exists p \in R . \mathrm{Irreducible} (p)\wedge (\forall x_{1} \in R . x_{1} \neq 0 \rightarrow (\exists n \in \Natural . \mathrm{Associated} (p ^ {n}, x_{1})))$
